> **SSOT first.** The single front-door tracker is [`SOURCE-OF-TRUTH.md`](./SOURCE-OF-TRUTH.md) (live cursor, status, build queue, founder rulings, findings, progress). This file is the per-feature master register + live In-Dev claims (machine/loop view) it points to, not the tracker to follow day-to-day.

> **What this is.** ONE canonical, at-a-glance, prioritized register of **every** feature - what is built, in dev, partial, paused, deferred, blocked, or open - each with a one-line "what it does", a category, and a priority, so any session can pick the top open item cold. This is the **front door** to status. Per-feature acceptance detail lives in [`archive/feature-backlog.md`](archive/feature-backlog.md); current-initiative build specs live in [`workspace-tenancy-and-monetization-plan.md`](./workspace-tenancy-and-monetization-plan.md) (G10) + [`byo-build-implementation-plan.md`](./byo-build-implementation-plan.md) (G11).

## STANDING RULE - REGISTER FIRST, THEN BUILD (non-negotiable, read before any feature work)

**No feature is built that is not tracked here first.** Every tool (Claude Code, Antigravity, Gemini, Lovable, a future session) must:

1. **`git pull origin main`**, then read this register. It is checked before any activity starts so a new or parallel session knows what is already in flight, deferred, or done, and does not collide or redo.
2. **REGISTER a new feature HERE FIRST.** Before any code, add a row with an **ID + Category + Priority** (and a one-line "What it does"). If it is not in this sheet with an ID, it does not get built.
3. **Respect the claims.** If a row is `🔨 In Dev`, another session may be on it - do not start it. Pick a different row or coordinate. Check the **Active claims** table first.
4. **On pickup:** flip Status to `🔨 In Dev (<tool>, YYYY-MM-DD)`, add a line to the **Active claims** table, and commit + push immediately so others see the claim - same commit, before you write feature code. (The literal `In Dev (` substring is preserved so the session-start hook can grep live claims.)
5. **On completion:** flip to `✅` (or `◐` if partial) with the date, remove the Active-claims line, and update the linked detail doc + `plan.md` §4 in the same unit of work (the closed-doc loop).
6. **On defer/pause/block:** flip to `⏭️` / `⏸️` / `🚧` and put the SHORT REASON in the **Comments** column (e.g. "deferred: post-PMF", "blocked: needs founder OAuth").

> Same shared-cursor discipline as the SSOT live cursor (section 0) and [`archive/feature-backlog.md`](archive/feature-backlog.md). When they disagree, fix all in the same commit.

### How to pick something up

Say **"pick `<ID>`"** (e.g. "pick WM-M1", "start SEN-01", "do F-IA-V4") and the agent resolves the ID here, reads the row, opens the linked detail (backlog / WM plan / BYO plan / feature doc), and builds. The IDs are stable and shared with [`archive/feature-backlog.md`](archive/feature-backlog.md).

### STANDING RULE - PROGRESS ACCOUNTING (keep the % live, never hand-guess it)

**This is how the "what percentage is done" question is answered at a glance, forever, without the number rotting.** The overall % and the per-category roll-up are **derived from the register rows**, not separately hand-kept, so they stay true as long as you follow this:

1. **Every row already carries its own % - read it off the status icon** via the **Item %** column in the Status legend below (✅ 100%, ◐/⏸️/🔨 50%, ⬜/⏭️/🚧 0%). That is the _individual-item_ completion. A ◐ partial may override its default 50% with an explicit `[~NN%]` token in its Comments when the remaining split is known (e.g. "remaining D4b ... `[~80%]`").
2. **The overall number is two figures, both shown in "At a glance":** _strict_ = fully-done rows / total; _weighted_ = sum of every row's Item % / total. Strict is the honest floor; weighted credits half-built work.
3. **Deferred rows stay in the denominator** - they are real planned scope, just not now. (For "% of active scope", subtract the deferred count from the denominator; the dashboard reports against all rows.)
4. **On ANY status change, recompute in the same commit.** Do not eyeball it - run the override-aware tally and paste the results in:
   ```
   awk -F'|' '/^\| [0-9]+ \|/{s=$3;gsub(/^[ \t]+|[ \t]+$/,"",s);c[s]++;t++; w=(s=="✅")?1:(s=="◐")?(match($0,/\[~[0-9]+%\]/)?substr($0,RSTART+2,RLENGTH-4)/100:0.5):(s=="⏸️"||s=="🔨")?0.5:0; W+=w} END{for(k in c)print k,c[k]; printf "total=%d strict=%.1f%% weighted=%.2f (%.1f%%)\n",t,(c["✅"]/t)*100,W,(W/t)*100}' docs/planning/feature-dashboard.md
   ```
   then update the "At a glance" strict/weighted/by-status/by-category numbers and the "Last recomputed" stamp.
5. **One number, one place.** [`SOURCE-OF-TRUTH.md`](./SOURCE-OF-TRUTH.md) (sections 0 + 2) shows the same headline % and points back here for the method. Never maintain a second independent figure.

### Status legend (each status carries a completion weight - the **Item %** is the per-item progress you read off any row)

| Mark | Meaning                                                                                                               | Item %                                       |
| ---- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------- |
| ✅   | **Done** - built, on `main`, verified (date where known)                                                              | **100%**                                     |
| 🔨   | **In Dev** - actively being built this/another session (see Active claims); keeps the literal `In Dev (` for the hook | **50%**                                      |
| ◐    | **Partial** - foundation built, real remaining work; Comments says what is left                                       | **50%** (override with `[~NN%]` in Comments) |
| ⏸️   | **Paused** - built or started but intentionally idle; reason in Comments                                              | **50%** (built but idle)                     |
| ⏭️   | **Deferred** - deliberately not now (gate/sequence reason in Comments)                                                | **0%**                                       |
| 🚧   | **Blocked** - cannot proceed until a dependency clears (founder action / KI); reason in Comments                      | **0%**                                       |
| ⬜   | **Open** - not started, ready to pick up                                                                              | **0%**                                       |

### Category legend (12 categories; mapped from the source groups)

| Category      | What it covers                                                                                              | Source group                         |
| ------------- | ----------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | ------------------------------------ |
| Foundational  | Core autonomous loop, memory engine, platform/auth/runtime infra                                            | G0 + G9                              |
| Sense         | Get real signal in, cluster it, keep it fresh                                                               | G1                                   |
| Decide        | Turn signal into governed decisions + specs (incl. the wedge)                                               | G2                                   |
| Build         | The autonomous Build -> QA -> Ship execution chain                                                          | G3 + G13 (build-driver / dispatch)   |
| Launch        | Ship to market, learn from outcomes, feed it back                                                           | G4                                   |
| Monetization  | Pricing, entitlements, tenancy/RBAC/billing, PLG                                                            | G5 + G10 (tenancy/RBAC/billing rows) |
| Credit        | The credit metering engine (WM-M10..WM-M16)                                                                 | G10 (credit rows)                    |
| Interop       | External agents/tools (MCP, A2A), team interop, export                                                      | G6                                   |
| Cockpit       | IA, observability, the operator's view of the machine                                                       | G7                                   |
| Governance    | Trust, safety, evals, drift, critic, incidents, humanization                                                | G8                                   |
| BYO           | Provider-agnostic repos + managed Cadence Cloud runtime                                                     | G11                                  |
| Observability | Analytics + failure detection + uptime/on-call/status (AFD initiative, 2026-06-25, doc-only, founder-gated) | G12                                  |

## ⚡ HOW TO PICK - the priority ranking (founder ruling 2026-06-21)

> **MANDATORY, non-negotiable.** The Master register below is **sorted by priority and ranked `#1, #2, #3 ...`** (`#1` = highest). **To pick, run `bash scripts/lane.sh next`** - it deterministically prints the next eligible item IDs (lowest Rank first: `⬜`/`◐` Tier-1/Tier-3, unclaimed, not DONE). **Do NOT judge, score, or decide what to pick - the command is the decision.** Build the first whose claim wins: `for id in $(bash scripts/lane.sh next); do bash scripts/lane.sh claim "$id" <laneN> "<globs>" && break; done`. A `◐` is PARTIAL, it has remaining work, so you **CONTINUE** it; **never skip a `◐` as "done" or "publish-verify-only"** (that false judgment idled all lanes with 60 items waiting). `Gated`/`Lovable`/`Done`/`Deferred` and chokepoint-pinned files are auto-excluded (by `next` and by the claim). Flip the row to `🔨 In Dev` on claim and `✅`/`◐` on finish, commit + push immediately on each. "Board dry" is real ONLY when `lane.sh next` prints nothing. Full law: [`../../AGENTS.md`](../../AGENTS.md) §3 + [`../operations/autonomous-build-loop.md`](../operations/autonomous-build-loop.md) §15.

### How priority is marked (the ONE uniform vocabulary - the Priority column is always exactly one of these)

| Priority class | Meaning                                                                                                                                 | In the pick-order?                                                         |
| -------------- | --------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------------- | -------------------------------------------------------------------------- |
| **Tier 1**     | Fundamentals / foundation / core features / core enablements / USPs (the moat, the loop, the wedge, chokepoint integrity). Build first. | yes, ranked first                                                          |
| **Tier 2**     | Design + eye-for-detail on the core surfaces. Founder-prompted, once, after the core is solid.                                          | yes, after Tier 1                                                          |
| **Tier 3**     | Non-essential / non-foundational (privacy, ops / reliability hygiene, enterprise-readiness, edge). Never outranks core.                 | yes, after Tier 2                                                          |
| **Tier 4**     | Final detailing + comprehensive polish (humanization sweep, lint, AI-trace, final design). Founder-prompted, last.                      | yes, last                                                                  |
| **Gated**      | Founder-gated; ranked in place but parked until the founder unblocks it (OAuth / secret / spend / taste).                               | skip until unblocked                                                       |
| **Lovable**    | Lovable-owned (billing / credit / pricing); built in parallel by Lovable.                                                               | no by default; **yes if the founder explicitly hands it back to an agent** |
| **Deferred**   | Cut / post-PMF / superseded.                                                                                                            | no                                                                         |
| **Done**       | Shipped.                                                                                                                                | no                                                                         |

**The tier law (build substance before surface; non-core last):** Tier 1 then Tier 2 then Tier 3 then Tier 4, in that strict order. This is why the rank puts every Tier-1 row above every Tier-2 row, and so on. It fixes the drift where the loop built Tier-3 ops hygiene while the Tier-1 moat work had no rows.

### Before you build a picked item: the sourcing gate + the autonomy doctrine (founder ruling 2026-06-21)

The Rank decides WHAT to build next; this decides HOW to source it, and WHO decides. Apply it to every item before writing code; the agent owns this call from its own web-grounded research (carry a greppable `BBI:` stamp on the deciding doc/PR). **The per-cluster verdict (what to build vs what to source elsewhere) lives in the [Sourcing Map](../strategy/sourcing-map.md): look up a picked item's cluster there FIRST, and do NOT build any sub-item the map marks obviated by a buy/integrate (wire the named provider behind the cluster's seam instead).** Canon: [`../strategy/build-buy-integrate.md`](../strategy/build-buy-integrate.md) + [`../../AGENTS.md`](../../AGENTS.md) §3.0c.

**The four sourcing decisions:**

- **BUILD** (in-house, from base) - the moat / USP: the typed decision ontology, the supersession engine, the Critic + precedent-salience, signal->ontology normalization, outcome->memory write-back, the system-of-record. **Never wrapped, never bought.** Default for anything that IS the differentiator.
- **BUY** (a commodity, always via the AI chokepoint `runtime.server.ts`, never called directly) - inference, embeddings, rerank, OCR, email, OAuth.
- **INTEGRATE** (a high-lock-in substrate, behind our OWN swappable seam, with a native default + graceful fallback) - e.g. the sandbox provider, a heavier graph engine only if we outgrow Postgres.
- **SELF-HOST** (open-source we run ourselves - the autonomy floor: a zero-external-paid-dep native default that must always hold) - e.g. SearXNG (`FIRECRAWL-FLOOR`), Postgres + pgvector for the graph.

**The Decision Brain verdict (the strongest opinion, since this was the open question):** the brain's INTELLIGENCE is **BUILD, in-house, forever** - the ontology, supersession (`DBR-1.5`), the Critic, the salience ranker. Never make the brain's reasoning a wrapper around someone else's product; that IS the moat. Its SUBSTRATE is **SELF-HOST/native** (Postgres + pgvector now), swappable to an **INTEGRATE'd** graph engine (Graphiti / Neo4j) behind a `GraphStore` seam only if scale demands; its embeddings + inference are **BUY** via the chokepoint; the Obsidian-style view **INTEGRATE**s a viz library (don't build a render engine). Net: the thinking is ours; only the plumbing is swappable.

**Autonomy doctrine (act on your own; escalate only when you must):**

- **ACT autonomously** - build the moat; buy a commodity via the chokepoint with NO new recurring spend; self-host an OSS native default; integrate behind a seam when a free/native default exists; any migration / server-fn needing no secret/spend/taste (gate it offline, flag for publish-verify).
- **THINK it through autonomously** (the agent decides via web research - do NOT ask the founder for this) - the sourcing call above, the architecture, the seam design, moat-vs-commodity. Carry the `BBI:` stamp on the deciding doc/PR.
- **ASK the founder** (escalate; mark the row `Gated`) - a new recurring SPEND; a SECRET / OAuth / paid-provider pick; a TASTE / positioning / pricing / product-tasting call; anything IRREVERSIBLE or OUTWARD-FACING (sending, publishing, the founder's accounts); a legal / policy call (data-retention scope, PII). When genuinely unsure whether it is a taste / spend / irreversible call, ask.

### The ranking is DERIVED - never hand-number it

The `#` Rank is computed, like the % tally. **To add or reprioritize an item:** add or edit its row with the right **Priority class** (and, for a Tier-1 item, place it among the Tier-1 rows in the order you want), then run **`python3 scripts/rerank-dashboard.py`**. It re-sorts the whole register by priority and renumbers `#1..N`, so a new high-priority item moves up and everything below it shifts down (nothing is left siloed, stuck on top, or stranded at the bottom). Then recompute the "At a glance" % (the awk in the Progress-accounting rule). Both run in the same commit as the change. The Tier-1 fine order lives in `scripts/rerank-dashboard.py` (the `T1` list).

### 🔁 Lovable-owned (mark `Lovable`; never a Claude pick - Lovable builds these in parallel)

> **TAKEOVER 2026-06-21 (founder ruling):** the **entire monetization + credit engine + admin category is now CLAUDE-owned, end to end (backend AND frontend) - NOT handed to Lovable.** Claude builds/fixes/tweaks all of it (existing UI may be tweaked; new backend gets new UI). The `Lovable` marks on the `M-C-*` / `WM-M*` / `BYO-P4` rows below are historical; treat them as Claude-owned now. Lovable's overnight build is the BASE; Claude owns it from here. Only pending founder action: provide live Stripe keys + flip `credits_enabled()` at go-live (see the go-live checklist).

Billing / Stripe: `M-C-PRICE`, `WM-M3`, `WM-M13`. Credit engine (already shipped dormant, do NOT extend; coordinate before live): `WM-M10`, `WM-M11`, `WM-M12`, `WM-M14`, `WM-M16`. Pricing / tier surfaces: `WM-M6`, `WM-M7`, `WM-M8`, `WM-M17`, `WM-M18`, `WM-M19`. Managed credits: `BYO-P4`. Admin Console + 06-20 follow-ups (Lovable's overnight build): `F-ADMIN-CONSOLE`, `ADM-DB`, `M-C-TOPUP-BUG`, `M-C-DEDUPE-WEBHOOK`, `M-C-PRICE-SYNC`, `M-C-BILLING-TESTS`, `M-C-DB-HYGIENE`. Split rows (Lovable owns the billing half only): `PLG` conversion leg, `R4` Plan/billing tab, `WM-F7` Plan/billing tab, `WM-M2` credit-pool/Stripe half. **STAYS product-infra (NOT Lovable):** every `WM-F*`, `WM-M1`, `WM-M4`, `WM-M5`, `WM-M9`, `WM-M15`, `M-C-EXPIRY`, `FND-AUTH`, `U6`. (The founder may ask Lovable to flip its rows to Done as that parallel work lands.)

## Active claims (who is on what, right now)

> **There is no separate status file - status lives in the register below (founder doctrine: no new docs).** Who is building what RIGHT NOW = the register rows whose Status is `🔨 In Dev`, plus the real-time CLI view `bash scripts/lane.sh board`. The committed register is the durable record; the **atomic ledger at `~/.cadence-parallel` is the real-time shared space** every session/worktree on this Mac sees instantly (no git needed). A background agent keeps this file fast-forwarded so your view stays current without a manual pull.
>
> **THE COLLISION GATE - two parts, both MANDATORY, both MECHANICAL (this is what stops two sessions doing the same work):**
>
> 1. **Claim before any work.** Run `bash scripts/lane.sh claim <ID> <laneN> "<globs>"` and proceed ONLY on success (exit 0). `HELD` = another lane holds it; `CONFLICT` = overlapping files; **`DONE` = another lane already COMPLETED it** (the ledger now remembers completions, so a finished item can never be re-picked) - any of these means STOP and pick the next item. **Never read, plan, or build before a successful claim.**
> 2. **Commit + push immediately on the two state changes** (founder ruling 2026-06-21): the instant you CLAIM (flip the register row to `🔨 In Dev (laneN, date)`) and the instant you finish (flip to `✅` and run `bash scripts/lane.sh done <ID>`, or `◐` + `lane.sh release <ID>` for a partial), commit with a WHY and **push right then** - so every other session sees it in git at the next pull, and `git status` stays clean. The ledger gives instant local visibility; the immediate push gives durable cross-session/cross-machine visibility.

New `src/lib/prompt-optimization.functions.ts` mines a prompt template's matching eval suite for graded failures (scoped to the template's current `active_version_id`, `status = 'failed'` only, not the broader `passed = false` which would wrongly count ungraded infra-error rows), gates on 2+ corroborating cases, and drafts a revised system prompt as a new `prompt_versions` row (`status: 'draft'`) via the existing versioned Prompt Studio schema - never touching `active_version_id`; a human publishes it from the pre-existing Prompt Studio UI, and the loop's existing `resolvePrompt` auto-pickup wires it in with zero code changes. New weekly cron `prompt-optimize-tick.ts` mirrors `house-rules-tick.ts`'s structure exactly. Fixed both: real columns only (`agent_slug`, `tool_name`, `decided_by`, `decided_at`, `status: "rejected"`, `rationale`), attribution via `artifact_lineage.created_by_agent` (child_kind='prd') for PRDs and `decisions.decided_by_agent_slug` directly for decisions; a human-authored artifact with no agent attribution writes no receipt. tsc 0, build clean. Ground-truth check first found the spec's premise had gone stale: Ask is no longer the full-page `/chat` route command-canvas.md assumed - OBS-12 (2026-07-02) retired that route and rebuilt Ask as `AskPanel.tsx`, a 420px right-docked ⌘J slide-over, and that same recent spec explicitly scoped out a second pane. Rather than reverse a one-day-old founder-relevant call silently, checked back: build blocks inside the existing panel, a new polling data channel, zero change to the locked `/api/chat` SSE contract, zero change to the panel's shape. **Shipped:** a new read-only `getAskMissionCanvas` (`src/lib/ask-canvas.functions.ts`) and three compact blocks (`src/components/obsidian/ask-canvas.tsx`) that render inside Ask's thread when a dispatched mission is running, replacing the old dead-end "Track the mission" link: **loop progress** (the last 5 `LoopStep`s, `StatusDot` + outcome-named status word, reusing `stepDotState`/`stepDescription` from `build-status.ts` unchanged), **memory-recall citations** (`memory_recall_log` joined to `agent_memory`, the FIRST live use of the previously-unused `Citation` primitive), and the **Critic verdict** (`studio_changesets.prd_id` → `prds.critic_review`, reusing `VerdictChip`). All three degrade to nothing when the mission genuinely has no data yet (no-filler law), never fabricated. **Adversarial 4-lens review (correctness / design-contract / scope-honesty, plus verify) caught and fixed 3 real design-contract issues + 1 minor pattern deviation before shipping:** a raw hardcoded hex (promoted to a new `--surface-recessed` token, `design-reference/obsidian-v3/tokens/colors.css` + `src/styles.css`); a `StatusDot` missing its required status word (a11y contract: status never relies on color alone); the reused `Citation` chip's superscript anatomy going visually inert outside inline prose (fixed with a visible mono-caps label per chip); and a genuine, non-obvious doctrine conflict the spec itself had flagged as unresolved ("Open questions for enrichment" #3, how the preview obeys the Ember system) - the Ask panel's own rule reserves its one ember slot for a proposed CTA, so a Critic REVISE verdict (ember-family tone) or a gated step could read as a second, misleading "you have something to do" signal; resolved conservatively by keeping the gate-state dot's ember (the step genuinely needs a human, matching canonical `StatusDot` behavior everywhere else) while downgrading only the advisory Critic REVISE tone to a neutral label inside this one context. `tsc --noEmit` 0 / `bun test` 2271/2271 pass (10 new: `runStatusLabel`'s outcome-naming, `hasCanvasContent`'s no-filler gating). **CMD-1 shipped 2026-07-03 (lane1), triggered by the founder asking why Ask was hard to find as a consumer.** Root cause was two latent bugs, not a missing visible-trigger affordance: (1) the palette's "Ask about this screen" row dispatched the open-ask event then unconditionally navigated to `/today` (a stale fallback from before `AskPanel.tsx` was mounted), silently destroying whatever screen the user meant to ask about; (2) typed text was wired only into a fake `About: <text>` context-chip label and was never actually sent as a question. Fixed in `ask-context.tsx` (dropped the dead `explicitContext` override; added `pendingIntent`/`runIntent`/`clearPendingIntent`), `AskPanel.tsx` (a one-shot effect auto-runs a queued intent through the existing unmodified `send()`/`/api/chat` pipeline), and `CommandPalette.tsx` (the Ask-opening row no longer navigates; a query matching nothing in JUMP/ACT/CATALOG now falls through to a live `Ask Cadence: "<query>"` row instead of a dead "Nothing by that name," which is CMD-1 itself). Deliberately did NOT add a persistent visible Ask button or a second coach mark to fix discoverability (`DESIGN-OBSIDIAN.md` bans new nav items/badges/banners, and this product keeps exactly one coach mark, ever). `tsc --noEmit` 0 / `bun test` 2299/2299 pass, zero regressions; verified by an independent adversarial-correctness pass and an independent design-compliance pass (both clean). **CMD-2 status (2026-07-04): BLOCKED ON DESIGN, do not auto-pick as the next slice.** Investigated before writing code: all three CMD-0 blocks (progress, memory citations, Critic verdict) are read-only telemetry, none is an editable artifact - the decision card/tradeoff matrix/roadmap diff the original vision imagined were never built, so click-to-target/highlight-to-edit has no surface to attach to. Building it now would mean inventing a new artifact type and editing surface with zero founder input. Founder call: leave it open, flag for a dedicated design session (open question #2 in the doc: which surface gets the canvas, new top-level or a mode inside an existing one). If you are an agent and `lane.sh next` points you at CMD-2: STOP, this is not a mechanical continuation of CMD-1. Docs: [`../features/command-canvas.md`](../features/command-canvas.md), [`../strategy/horizon-bets.md`](../strategy/horizon-bets.md). |

| 143 | ✅ | RF-01 | Outcome attribution breadth | Chain the observers that already run into provisional outcomes: outcome-tick ship detection feeds the Historian draft (`suggestOutcomeVerdict`), PostHog usage deltas (SEN-05 `product_analytics` + ice-adjust) and the BYO-P3 changeset→PRD join attribute to decisions; confidence-tiered, one-click confirm, low-confidence queues as suggestions | Knowledge | Done | 2026-07-02 | **SHIPPED 2026-07-02 (lane4).** New `src/lib/outcome-suggestion.server.ts` (`generateOutcomeSuggestion`) chains the three existing observers into a confidence-tiered `prds.outcome_suggestion` (new jsonb column, `20260702233000_rf01_outcome_suggestions.sql`, mirrors `prds.outcome`'s existing convention — no new RLS needed): the Historian draft (`draftOutcomeVerdict`, extracted from `suggestOutcomeVerdict`'s handler so the same drafting core serves both the human-triggered button and this cron path), SEN-05 `product_analytics` usage deltas (same 30-day window + column shape `ice-adjust.server.ts`'s `autoAdjustIce` reads, so the two signals never disagree), and the BYO-P3 changeset join (`pickChangesetForPrd` + `shouldPublishChangelog`, reused not reimplemented). Confidence = `0.5*usageSignal + 0.3*changesetSignal + 0.2*predictionSignal` (0.6 threshold for "high"); never fabricates a suggestion with zero signal, never overwrites a human-recorded outcome (`.is("outcome", null)` write guard, race-safe — Postgres evaluates it atomically so a `recordOutcome` landing mid-draft is never clobbered), and only re-drafts a stale low-confidence suggestion when its underlying basis has actually changed (skips the AI call otherwise — bounded cost, never re-spends on an unchanged signal). Wired into `outcome-tick.ts`'s existing hourly cron as a second pass (oldest-shipped-first, capped batch, best-effort per PRD). `OutcomeCard.tsx`: a high-confidence suggestion surfaces a one-click "Confirm outcome" (fires the same `recordOutcome` mutation as the manual form, reading current state so an in-flight edit is never dropped); a low-confidence one pre-fills the manual form for review. The pre-fill is touch-guarded (a ref tracks whether the operator has started editing *this* PRD's form) so a suggestion arriving while the card is open never clobbers in-progress typing. Adversarial review (fresh-eyes code-reviewer): 1 real bug found + fixed (the touch-guard race above), 3 minor findings fixed (re-drafting collapsed into one mutation instead of two divergent record paths; a basis-unchanged short-circuit on the AI re-draft; oldest-shipped-first ordering on the new batch query). Gates: `tsc --noEmit` 0 (4 pre-existing unrelated stripe-package errors only) / `bun test` 2064 pass (7 new `outcome-suggestion.server.test.ts` pinning the confidence formula), 1 pre-existing fail + 1 pre-existing error both unrelated (missing `stripe` package / missing local Supabase env vars). **Not yet live:** the new migration is code-complete and gate-verified but not yet applied to the live database (lands on the founder's next publish, same as other recently-shipped migrations). **Addendum 2026-07-10 (main session, e3b82676): the loop now closes INTO the ranking - recorded outcomes move the order of NEW bets.** The deterministic DEC-RANK chain had zero memory input; a new comparator tier (after the Critic's verdict, before raw signal volume) folds each theme's decisive outcome record in via the pure `outcomeSupportFromCounts` (validated lifts, missed sinks, capped at 3 per side so memory advises the human's ICE, never overrules it). Wired in both consumers (Discover queue from learnings it already fetched - zero new queries; the server-side best-bet pick feeding Today's judgment lane via a theme_id embed on the existing learnings reads) and spoken in the rationale ("outcomes on this theme run proven / have missed"). 7 new ranking tests; default callback keeps any unwired caller byte-identical. |
| 144 | ✅ | RF-02 | Outcome-weighted retrieval (the keystone) | Add an outcome + decay term to the `match_agent_memory` RPC ORDER BY (fields already on the row: importance, `last_used_at`, `metadata->>'verdict'`) so a validated precedent outranks a similar-but-refuted one; upgrades the loop, Ambient Precedent, the Critic, and chat in one migration | Knowledge | Done | 2026-07-02 | **SHIPPED 2026-07-03 (lane4).** One migration, `20260703000000_rf02_outcome_weighted_retrieval.sql` — SQL-only, no pinned-file edits, no signature/return-shape change, so every caller (`memory.server.ts`, `decision-precedent.server.ts`, `brain/novelty.server.ts`, and transitively the loop/Ambient Precedent/Critic/chat) is upgraded by this one `CREATE OR REPLACE FUNCTION`. Reranks by `(distance) + outcome_term + importance_term + decay_term`: `metadata->>'verdict'` gives 'validated' -0.05 / 'missed' +0.05 (mixed/none neutral), `importance` (1-5) gives ±0.01 per point off center, and a recency-decay term reuses the exact `exp(-ageHours/72)` half-life `brain/score.ts` already uses elsewhere in the codebase — all small nudges (max swing +0.09/-0.07) that rerank among already-similar candidates, never override genuine semantic relevance. TWO-STAGE query (a `candidates` CTE doing the exact prior `order by embedding <=> query_embedding limit N` shape, widened to `greatest(match_count*4, 20)`, then an outer query reranking only that small pool) so the HNSW index-assisted ANN top-K scan is preserved — a database-specialist review flagged that a bare compound-expression ORDER BY would defeat index pushdown, fixed before shipping. That same review also caught a real caller-side bug: `brain/novelty.server.ts` assumed the RPC's row `[0]` was always the max-similarity match, an assumption this rerank breaks (row `[0]` is now the best COMBINED score, not necessarily max raw similarity) — fixed by finding the max-similarity row explicitly (`reduce` over the array) instead of indexing positionally; the sibling `match_themes` caller in the same function got the identical defensive fix for consistency even though its own RPC ordering is unaffected. `decision-precedent.server.ts` was confirmed unaffected (it re-sorts by raw similarity client-side over a wide pool). Verified the security-critical WHERE clause (user/workspace/account scoping, `is_account_member`/`is_workspace_member`, expiry, embedding-not-null, agent_slug/scope) is byte-identical to the prior migration — zero RLS/tenant-isolation change, confirmed by a line-level diff, not just inspection. No live DB access in this environment to run `EXPLAIN ANALYZE`; offline `bun scripts/lint-migrations.ts` apply-safety lint passes 0 fatal errors. RETURNING` so the importance clamp (1..5, matching `agent_memory`'s own CHECK) can never race. `src/lib/ai/memory.server.ts` (pinned, founder-authorized): new `logMemoryRecall` next to `touchMemory` — writes one recall_log row per recalled memory, keyed by the run's **trace_id**, not any single event_id, because one recall's lines are baked into the system prompt once and reused by every `callModel` call in `executeLoop`'s per-step loop (which was deliberately NOT touched — only the two one-shot recall call sites were). `src/lib/ai/loop.server.ts` (pinned): the `recallMemory` wrapper now returns `{lines, refs}` instead of discarding refs; both call sites (`runAgentLoop` fresh dispatch, `resumeAgentLoop`'s cold-resume branch) call `logMemoryRecall` right after recall. `src/lib/feedback.functions.ts`'s `submitFeedback` is the behavioral consumer: on a rating, atomically claims this trace's still-`'ignored'` recall rows (`UPDATE ... WHERE outcome='ignored' ... RETURNING`, not a SELECT-then-UPDATE, so a concurrent second claim can't double-apply) into 'used' (rating>0) or 'contradicted' (rating<0), then calls `bump_memory_importance` for each distinct memory touched — feeding RF-02's ranking through the importance term it already reads, no RF-02 change needed. A database-specialist review of the first draft caught one real race (the original SELECT-then-UPDATE claim could double-apply under concurrent feedback) and I caught one myself before it shipped (the importance-bump RPC was drafted `SECURITY DEFINER`, which would have let any authenticated user bump ANY memory's importance by id, bypassing `agent_memory`'s own RLS — fixed to run as invoker so the existing ownership policy gates it, exactly as if the caller wrote the UPDATE directly). Confirmed additive-only on both chokepoint files: `executeLoop`'s per-step loop, checkpoint save/restore, and `recalledMemories`'s `string[]` shape are byte-identical; the only other caller of the local `recallMemory` wrapper besides the two updated sites doesn't exist (verified by repo-wide grep). Founder asked directly why it was stuck and gave explicit authority to seed data where needed to close it fully. Wired 2 of the 3 named surfaces with genuinely real data (zero fabrication): Engine Room **Spend/TREND** (`SpendRoom.tsx`) gets a teal `Sparkline` fed by `getAnalyticsOverview`'s already-fetched `daily` cost series (zero new query); Engine Room **Quality/SCORE** (`QualityRoom.tsx`) gets a `Sparkline` fed by a new `scoreTrend` field on `computeEvalHealth` (`src/lib/evals/health.ts`, chronological avg_score of the last 10 scored runs, real run history only); Brain's **stat trio** (`BrainStatTrio.tsx`) gets a small `Sparkline` fed by a new `decisionsTrend` field on `computeImpactLedger` (`src/lib/pm-impact.ts`, real decisions bucketed into 8 weekly buckets by actual `created_at`, rendered only when at least one bucket is non-zero). **Build's mission cost view stays untouched, by design.** `MissionSlideOver.tsx`'s own header comment already documents the pre-existing, pre-authorized data gap (`LoopStep` carries no per-step cost, only the prototype's static sample does) and says explicitly not to fabricate it - the same "never fabricate" law `BrainStatTrio.tsx`'s own header invokes for its ICE cell - so this is the one deliberate, honest exclusion, not an oversight. **Seeded real historical demo data** (`supabase/migrations/20260703160000_obs15_demo_chart_seed.sql`, idempotent, demo-scoped to both `demo@redcadence.app`/`demo2@redcadence.app` via `NOT EXISTS` guards on a distinguishing tag per table, same columns the base seed already writes, no fabricated field, no schema change): the base seed clusters its 18 `ai_events` within 18 hours and writes exactly 1 `eval_runs` row, too few days/points for a sparkline to draw a real line. Added 8 more days of `ai_events`, 5 more `eval_runs` on the existing suite (avg_score climbing 68→88 over 2 weeks), and 5 more `decisions` backdated across 8 weeks, so the demo accounts show a genuine trend instead of a flat or empty chart. **Adversarial 3-lens review (data honesty / spec-compliance-restraint-budget / migration-safety) caught 2 real bugs, both fixed before closing:** (1) the seeded `eval_runs` rows omitted `created_at`, the column `scoreTrend` actually sorts on - a single multi-row `INSERT` would have collapsed all 5 rows to the same `now()` timestamp and lost the intended chronological climb; fixed by setting `created_at` explicitly to match `started_at` per row. (2) the new Spend sparkline plotted `getAnalyticsOverview`'s sparse `daily` array by index, which omits (not zero-fills) a day with no events, so a quiet day in a real workspace would render as if adjacent to its neighbors, implying a smooth trend across a gap that never happened; fixed with a new pure, tested `zeroFillDaily` helper (`src/lib/engine-room-glance.ts`) that zero-fills to a fixed 7-day window before the sparkline ever sees the data. Gates: `tsc --noEmit` 0 · `bun test` 2211/2211 pass (13 new: `scoreTrend`'s chronological ordering/cap/empty-safety, `decisionsTrend`'s weekly bucketing/window-drop/malformed-timestamp handling, `zeroFillDaily`'s gap-day correctness, `deriveBrainStats`'s trend passthrough) · humanized-output clean. (2) `src/lib/mcp/auth.server.ts` — `verifyMcpToken(authHeader): {workspaceId, scopes}` — hashes incoming bearer token (SHA-256), looks up `mcp_tokens`, returns workspace_id + scopes; throws 401 if missing/expired/not found. (3) `src/lib/mcp/tools.server.ts` — exports `MCP_TOOLS` array (7 tools with JSON Schema input defs) + `dispatchMcpTool(toolName, args, workspaceId, scopes)`: routes to correct query fn; enforces `write:signal` scope gate on `ingest_signal`; pipes all inbound args through `screensForInjection()` (existing util in `src/lib/security/injection.server.ts`); each query re-checks `workspace_id` at the DB query layer (belt + suspenders over RLS). Tool implementations: `query_decisions` → existing `getDecisions()` fn; `list_missions` → existing `listMissions()`; `get_workspace_context` → workspace + active product summary; `list_opportunities` → existing `listOpportunities()`; `get_trust_ledger` → existing `getLedgerEntries()`; `query_knowledge` → existing brain/knowledge query; `ingest_signal` → existing `ingestSignal()` gated on `write:signal` scope. (4) `src/lib/mcp/audit.server.ts` — `logMcpCall(workspaceId, toolName, actorTokenId)` → inserts into `admin_audit_log` with `target_kind='mcp_tool'`. (5) `src/routes/api/mcp.ts` — POST `/api/mcp`; follows `src/routes/api/chat.ts` pattern (`createFileRoute` + `server.handlers.{POST, OPTIONS}`); calls `verifyMcpToken`, `dispatchMcpTool`, `logMcpCall`; returns JSON-RPC 2.0 response `{jsonrpc:"2.0", result:{content:[{type:"text",text:"<markdown>"}]}}`. (6) `src/lib/mcp-tokens.functions.ts` — `createMcpToken(name, scopes[])`, `listMcpTokens()`, `revokeMcpToken(id)` TanStack server functions; `createMcpToken` generates a random token, stores its SHA-256 hash, returns the raw token ONCE (never again). (7) `src/components/settings/McpTokensPanel.tsx` — Settings card under Connections tab: "Create token" (name + scope checkboxes), token list (name / scopes / last-used / created / revoke button), one-time display of the raw token on creation (copy-to-clipboard). (8) Wire `McpTokensPanel` into `src/routes/_authenticated.settings.tsx` Connections group. (9) Update `public/llms.txt` — change "MCP Server: coming soon" to "available at POST /api/mcp (bearer token required — generate in Settings > Connections > API tokens)". (10) Update `src/routes/api/public/agent-card.ts` (/.well-known/agent.json) — add `mcp_endpoint: "/api/mcp"` and `mcp_tools: [...]` to the agent card JSON. Gate: `MCP_ENABLED=true` env var (dormant-by-design until founder flips). The leak-hunt surfaced a REAL latent landmine on the `accounts` "account members read" policy: source `20260619210000` wrote `m.account_id = id` intending `accounts.id`, but inside the subquery `account_members` is in scope and also has an `id`, so Postgres bound the unqualified `id` to the INNER row -> stored as `m.account_id = m.id` (a self-compare). DEAD today (0 of 6 account_members rows self-equal, contributes nothing under PERMISSIVE OR-ing) but a footgun: any future row with `id == account_id` flips the uncorrelated EXISTS to always-true -> full `accounts`-table exposure. Remaining (separate infra, repo-wide pattern): DB-integration tests for the webhook DB-writes + voucher redemption (no DB-mock harness in CI). · **2026-06-22 (Lane 1): webhook decision-logic gap CLOSED.** Extracted the two revenue-critical inline rules from `payments/webhook.ts` into pure, tested `billing-tier.ts` fns the 3 subscription handlers now share (no drift): `effectiveTierForStatus(tier,status)` (keep paid access through `past_due` dunning, downgrade to `free` on real termination) + `subscriptionStatusGrantsCredits(status)` (only `active`/`trialing` mint credits; `past_due` keeps access but no fresh allowance). +7 tests (billing-tier 6→13); behavior-identical extraction, tsc 0, billing cluster 69 pass. So the webhook "tier map" coverage the row flagged is now real, harness-free. Remaining narrows to: DB-write integration (upsert/update side-effects) + voucher redemption, both needing a Supabase-mock harness. · **2026-06-22 (Lane 1, cont.): webhook decision/EXTRACTION layer extracted + tested.** The other untested mis-bill-risk surface in `payments/webhook.ts` was the inline event-shaping logic. Extracted into pure `src/lib/billing-webhook.ts` (`resolvePriceLookup` lookup_key→external_id→id fallback; `resolveTopupCredits` catalog→legacy-map; `unixSecondsToIso` no-fake-epoch; `isTopupCheckout`; `isRenewalInvoice` subscription_cycle-only no-double-grant; `resolvePeriod` line-item-over-sub) + 13 tests; wired back into all handlers behavior-identical (also corrected the old `resolvePriceLookup: string` type-lie). The webhook's full decision+extraction layer is now tested + DRY. tsc 0 / suite 979. · **2026-06-22 (Lane 1, cont.²): webhook PAYLOAD-ASSEMBLY layer extracted + tested — the webhook's WHOLE TS-testable surface is now covered.** Extracted the subscription DB-row assembly into pure `buildSubscriptionUpsert(sub,env,nowIso)` + `buildSubscriptionUpdate(sub,nowIso)` (every field mapped from the Stripe event; `nowIso` injected for determinism; required-string fields asserted to match the table Insert type, behavior-identical to the inline `any`-based payload) + 4 tests (field-mapping, cancel_at_period_end default, line-item-over-sub period fallback, the update subset excludes user_id/customer/environment); wired into `handleSubscriptionCreated`/`handleSubscriptionUpdated` behavior-identical. Now the webhook's decision + extraction + payload layers are ALL pure-tested + DRY (a wiring/field-map bug can't slip in untested). tsc 0 / suite **1003** (+4). · **2026-06-22 (Lane 1): ✅ CLOSED.** Final buildable slice: extracted the top-up per-cycle cap (`monthlyGrant>0 ? *2 : 5000`) — which was DUPLICATED across `createTopUpCheckout` + `getMyCreditsView` (a runtime-fatal drift risk the verifier flagged: UI could show a different cap than the backend enforces) — into one pure `topUpCycleCap()` in billing-tier.ts, wired both TS sites + the early-return to it, and added a SQL↔TS parity guard (`topup-cap-sql-parity.test.ts`) that parses the `apply_topup_credits` migration's `_cap := case … end` and fails if the SQL formula/fallback ever drifts from the TS helper. +5 cap tests + 4 parity tests; billing/credit cluster **127 pass**, tsc 0, lint clean. The whole **TS-unit-testable** monetization surface is now covered (decision + extraction + payload + cap layers, all pure + DRY + SQL-parity-guarded). **Remaining is NOT TS-unit-testable (founder-env / infra, NOT a build gap):** the live-DB round-trip + the SQL `redeem_voucher`/~40 admin RPCs need a live Supabase or a chained-mock harness that does not exist in CI — these are integration tests gated on the founder's live DB, not autonomous work. DONE. |

## Status reconciliation note

Statuses here are reconciled from [`SOURCE-OF-TRUTH.md`](./SOURCE-OF-TRUTH.md) (sections 0/2/3, the live cursor + status + build queue) and [`archive/feature-backlog.md`](archive/feature-backlog.md) (granular ledger). Where docs conflict on a "done" claim, trust this file + the live code, then fix the others in the same commit. Granular acceptance criteria + "how to use / verify" blocks live in [`archive/feature-backlog.md`](archive/feature-backlog.md); milestone exit criteria + the founder pickup list live in [`SOURCE-OF-TRUTH.md`](./SOURCE-OF-TRUTH.md) (sections 2-4); open bugs live in [`known-issues.md`](./known-issues.md). F-SHARE is one feature listed under both Decide (G2) and Monetization (G5); it is a single row here (Decide, shipped) per the register-one-row-per-ID rule.
